<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every nonnegative integer <i>n</i>, we construct a Smith–Toda complex $`V(n)`$ at some prime <i>p</i> depending on <i>n</i>. It is a finite <i>p</i>-local spectrum whose Brown–Peterson homology is $`\mathrm{BP}_*/(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n)`$ with its canonical comodule structure and generator in degree zero. Each listed generator is killed to its first power.

- **claim-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $n\geq0$, there exist a prime $p$ and a finite $p$-local spectrum $X$ such that $$\mathrm{BP}_*X\cong\mathrm{BP}_*/(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n)$$ as graded $\mathrm{BP}_*\mathrm{BP}$-comodules, with the canonical quotient coaction and generator in degree zero.
  - Explanation: Existence of a finite Smith–Toda complex at every height, with the prime allowed to depend on the height; the quotient of Brown–Peterson homology by the first-power regular sequence is realized as a finite local spectrum with its canonical coaction.
- **claim-ultraproduct** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-ultraproduct)): The category $\mathcal C$ is stable and symmetric monoidal, with finite stable constructions and tensor products computed primewise. If $X=[(X_p)]$ and $Y=[(Y_p)]$, then $$\label{eq:found-mapping-spectrum} \mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal C}(X,Y) \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U\in\mathcal U}\prod_{p\in U} \mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{(p)}}(X_p,Y_p).$$ Every mapping spectrum is rational, and the endomorphism algebra of the tensor unit is $HD$. The ring $D$ contains $\mathbb Q$, and $\varpi$ is a nonzerodivisor.* *Objects, arrows, and homotopies forming a diagram indexed by a finite simplicial set can be represented simultaneously on a large set of primes. The same holds for specified finite cofiber diagrams. Sequence objects become compact in $\mathrm{Ind}(\mathcal C)$.
  - Explanation: The ultraproduct of local spectra over the odd primes is stable and symmetric monoidal, with rational mapping spectra and unit endomorphisms given by the ultraproduct ring.
- **claim-rectification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-rectification)): Let $R$ be a discrete commutative ring containing $\mathbb Q$, and let $\Gamma$ be a discrete abelian group. Commutative algebras in $\Gamma$-graded $HR$-module spectra admit strict commutative differential graded $R$-algebra models, with no boundedness requirement on chain degree. This description is compatible with diagrams and with derived base change under specified scalar algebras.
  - Explanation: Over a rational discrete ring, graded commutative algebra objects have strict differential graded models compatible with diagrams and base change.
- **claim-free-null** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-free-null)): Let $B$ be a $\Gamma$-graded commutative differential graded algebra over a discrete ring $R\supset\mathbb Q$, and let $x\in B_\eta$ be a cycle of chain degree $r$. The free extension $$B//x:=B[z],\qquad \|z\|_{\mathrm{chain}}=r+1,\quad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(z)=\eta,\quad \partial z=x$$ models the derived algebra pushout universally adjoining a null of $x$. In contrast, write $B/x$ for the underlying module cofiber of multiplication by $x$, with its degree and weight shifts.* *When $r=0$, there is an identification of underlying $B$-modules $$\label{eq:found-null-cofiber} B//x\simeq B/x.$$ It uses the specified universal null $z$. Consequently, an algebra map from $B//x$ defined by a map from $B$ and a specified null of the image of $x$ induces the corresponding map of module cofibers using that null multiplied by the base input.
  - Explanation: Adjoining a free variable with prescribed boundary models the derived pushout killing a cycle, and in chain degree zero it matches the module cofiber via the specified null.
- **claim-scalar-pushouts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-scalar-pushouts)): The free cycle and disk algebras over $R$ are obtained by extension of scalars from those over $\mathbb Q$. Thus the cell in Lemma <a href="#found:free-null" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:free-null">4</a> also has its stated universal property when considered merely as an attachment over rational spectra. More generally, a pushout of a span of algebras under common scalars is also the pushout of that span without separately imposing those scalars: the map from the middle algebra already supplies the common scalar map and its compatibility. We will use this observation for power operations that can change the action of $D$. Comparisons involving specified nulls are transported together with their specified homotopies along equivalences.
  - Explanation: Cell attachments over the larger scalar ring come from extension of scalars, so universal properties persist over rational spectra.
- **claim-picard-grading** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-picard-grading)): Let $\mathcal D$ be a stable symmetric monoidal category with rational mapping spectra, and let $\Gamma$ be a finite free abelian group with a chosen basis. Suppose the desired invertible object for each basis element is a tensor square of an invertible object. These objects extend to a symmetric monoidal grading of $\mathcal D$ by the discrete group $\Gamma$.* *For two such gradings, prescribed equivalences on the basis extend to a monoidal equivalence of gradings. The construction can be made relative to any specified subset of the basis.
  - Explanation: Square-root invertible objects over a basis extend to a coherent symmetric monoidal grading by a finite free abelian group, uniquely up to prescribed basis equivalences.
- **claim-mapping-pairings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-mapping-pairings)): The constructions $h_\Gamma$ are exact componentwise and possess coherent lax symmetric monoidal pairings. On algebraic inputs they give graded $D$-algebras and their modules. The pairings are balanced over $D$ whenever its action is supplied by trivial sphere scalars. They also respect additional diagram indices, including filtrations.
  - Explanation: The normalized mapping functors are exact and carry coherent lax monoidal pairings, giving graded algebras and modules over the ultraproduct ring.
- **claim-whole-systems** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-whole-systems)): For graded inputs we use an ultraproduct of categories of *whole* primewise graded systems. Evaluation at any fixed index gives a functor into $\mathcal C$. Primewise graded pairings then give the diagrams and pairings used in Lemma <a href="#found:mapping-pairings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:mapping-pairings">7</a>, compatibly with restriction to large sets. One may first pull these systems back along prime-dependent additive maps from a common indexing lattice. This only uses a functor from the ultraproduct of whole systems to systems in $\mathcal C$; it does not identify these categories or assert that every diagram in $\mathcal C$ has such a lift. The same distinction applies to group actions, filtrations, and module structures. The simultaneous coherent sphere grading was chosen only in $\mathcal C$. An individual sphere or cell shift used primewise is simply the sphere of the indicated degree.
  - Explanation: Graded inputs are modeled by whole primewise systems, with a functor to the ultraproduct category that is not claimed to be an identification.
- **claim-finite-cell-morita** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-finite-cell-morita)): The functor $h_\Omega$ induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence between $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{cell}}$ and the category of finite-cell graded $A$-modules. For $X,Y\in\mathcal C$, the natural comparison $$\label{eq:found-tensor-comparison} h_\Omega(X)\otimes_A h_\Omega(Y) \longrightarrow h_\Omega(X\otimes Y)$$ is an equivalence whenever either input is in $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{cell}}$. These comparisons preserve units, compositions, and symmetric multi-input products. In particular, a commutative $A$-algebra whose underlying module is finite-cell determines a commutative algebra in $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: Finite-cell objects of the ultraproduct category correspond symmetrically monoidally to finite-cell graded modules over the endomorphism algebra of the graded unit.
- **claim-koszul-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-found-koszul-realization)): Fix $0\leq r\leq n$. Suppose cycles $b_h$ of chain degree zero and weight $w_h$, for $0\leq h\leq r$, have been chosen successively in the strict algebras $$\label{eq:found-exterior-tower} \begin{gathered} A[e_0,\ldots,e_{j-1}],\qquad \|e_h\|_{\mathrm{chain}}=1,\quad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(e_h)=w_h,\\ \partial e_h=b_h\in A[e_0,\ldots,e_{h-1}], \qquad b_0=\varpi u_0. \end{gathered}$$ For $0\leq j\leq r+1$, these algebras determine commutative algebras $Y_j\in\mathcal C$, with $Y_0=\mathbf 1$. For $0\leq j\leq r$, the cycle $b_j$ represents a map $S(w_j)\to Y_j$, and there is a cofiber sequence $$\label{eq:found-cofiber-tower} S(w_j)\otimes Y_j\xrightarrow{\,b_j\cdot-\,}Y_j \longrightarrow Y_{j+1},$$ whose second map is the algebra map adjoining the null $e_j$. For $M=[(\mathrm{MU}_{(p)})_p]$ and $0\leq j\leq r+1$, there are compatible equivalences $$\label{eq:found-mu-base-change} h_\Omega(M\otimes Y_j) \simeq h_\Omega(M)\otimes_A A[e_0,\ldots,e_{j-1}].$$
  - Explanation: A tower of exterior extensions by degree-zero cycles realizes as a tower of cofibers of multiplication maps in the ultraproduct category, with compatible base change to complex cobordism.
- **claim-localizations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-power-localizations)): Let $\mathcal E$ be the symmetric monoidal stable localization of $\mathrm{Ind}(\mathcal C_G)$ by the localizing tensor ideal generated by $G_+\otimes X$, for all $X\in\mathcal C_G$. For $b\geq1$, put $$E_b=G^{*b},\qquad \widetilde E_b=\mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}(E_{b+}\longrightarrow\mathbf 1).$$ The image of every object of $\mathcal C_G$ is compact in $\mathcal E$, and, for $X,Y\in\mathcal C_G$, $$\label{power:join-formula} \mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal E}(X,Y) \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_b\mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal C_G}(X,\widetilde E_b\otimes Y).$$ There is a further symmetric monoidal localization $\mathcal E'$ in which $a_2$ is invertible. Its localization of an object $Y$ is represented in $\mathcal E$ by $$\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{s\geq0} \bigl(Y\xrightarrow{a_2}T^2Y\xrightarrow{a_2}T^4Y \longrightarrow\cdots\bigr).$$ Mapping out of a sequence object commutes with this telescope.
  - Explanation: Two successive localizations of naive equivariant spectra, one killing induced objects and one inverting the Euler class, are computed by explicit colimits whose maps out of compact objects commute with the telescope.
- **claim-prime-to-p** (theorem, [source](#stmt-power-prime-to-p)): If a finite group $H$ has order prime to $p$, every naive $H$-spectrum in $\mathrm{Sp}_{(p)}$ is a retract of the induction of its underlying spectrum. In particular, homotopy fixed points preserve underlying lower connectivity bounds for such actions.
  - Explanation: Actions of groups of order prime to the prime are retracts of induced objects, so homotopy fixed points preserve lower connectivity bounds.
- **claim-exactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-power-exactness)): The composite $N:\mathcal C\to\mathcal E'$ is exact. More precisely, for a map $f:A_0\to A_1$, form the canonical comparison in $\mathcal C_G$ $$\kappa_f:\mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}(NA_0\longrightarrow NA_1) \longrightarrow N(\mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}f).$$ Its fiber is annihilated by one multiplication by $a_2$, simultaneously at all primes.
  - Explanation: The permutation power becomes exact after Euler inversion because the comparison fiber is killed by a single Euler map uniformly in the prime.
- **claim-euler-inversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-power-graded-localization)): Replacing $\mathcal E$ by $\mathcal E'$ in Equation <a href="#power:B" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="power:B">[power:B]</a> gives the algebra $B(X)[t^{-1}]$. At $\lambda=m=0$, trivial action gives a natural algebra map on the $\delta$-grading $$h_\Lambda(X)\longrightarrow B(X)_{0,*,0}.$$ We call this the scalar action in the $\delta$ direction.
  - Explanation: Passing to the Euler-inverted category corresponds to algebraic inversion of the Euler parameter in the graded mapping algebra, with a scalar action in the ordinary direction.
- **claim-raw-power** (construction, [source](#stmt-power-operation)): For a sequence of actual primewise $\Lambda$-graded commutative algebras, there is a natural map of graded commutative algebras over rational spectra $$\label{power:raw-operation} P_X:h_\Lambda(X)\longrightarrow B(X)[t^{-1}]_{*,0,0}.$$ On a represented degree-zero class, its value is obtained by taking the permuted $p$-fold smash and then multiplying the $p$ factors. In particular, that value has this represented class in $B(X)$ before Euler inversion. No $D$-linearity is asserted for $P_X$.
  - Explanation: The unnormalized power is a natural algebra map into the Euler-inverted mapping algebra, given on represented classes by the permuted smash followed by multiplication; no linearity over the larger scalar ring is claimed.
- **claim-acyclic-sphere** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-acyclic-sphere)): For every odd prime $p$, the space $Z_p^{\mathrm{orb}}=S(V)_{hG_p}$ has the $p$-local homology of a point. Its stabilized augmentation to the $p$-local sphere is an equivalence.
  - Explanation: The Borel orbit space of the unit sphere has the local homology of a point, so its augmentation to the local sphere is an equivalence.
- **claim-even-trivializations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-even-trivializations)): For every even integer $r$, including negative integers, the local $p$-local sphere of $rV$ over $Z_p^{\mathrm{orb}}$ is trivializable with fiber degree $r(p-1)$. Consequently there are coherent $J^\vee$-module equivalences $$\label{layer:oriented-W} J^\vee\otimes W_i\simeq J^\vee\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}S(Fi) \qquad(i\in I).$$ They may be chosen to agree with the given trivial identifications on the $\delta$ summand, and they remain equivalences after localization.
  - Explanation: Even multiples of the representation sphere trivialize over the Borel orbit space, giving coherent module equivalences between twisted and trivially acted spheres.
- **claim-norm-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-norm-model)): There is a natural cofiber sequence $$\label{eq:norm-triangle} \operatorname{Part}(U)\longrightarrow \operatorname{Full}(U)\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal E}(\mathbf 1,U).$$ The first map is projection from a join stage followed by the norm. The norm is an equivalence on induced spectra and on their finite stable extensions. On underlying fibers, fiber inclusion followed by norm and evaluation is the sum of the group actions.
  - Explanation: A natural cofiber sequence relates partial orbits, full homotopy fixed points and maps from the unit in the localized category, via the norm.
- **claim-partial-thom** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-partial-thom)): Let $Z=(Z_p)_p$ be any sequence of $p$-local spectra, without connectivity or boundedness assumptions. Let $r=(r_p)_p$ be a sequence of integers of fixed parity. If $r$ is odd, then $$\operatorname{Part}(T^r\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Z)=0.$$ If $r$ is even, inclusion of a fiber point at the identity vertex of $E_b$ induces an equivalence $$\prod_{\mathcal U}(T_p^{r_p}\otimes Z_p)\xrightarrow{\simeq} \operatorname{Part}(T^r\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Z),$$ where the source has forgotten the group action.
  - Explanation: Partial Thom spectra with arbitrary trivially acted coefficients vanish for odd twists and reduce to the underlying fiber for even twists.
- **claim-layer-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-coefficient-algebra)): For every ordinary sequence $Z$, external multiplication is an equivalence $$\label{layer:external-product} \mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal E}(\mathbf 1,J^\vee)\otimes_D\mathcal H(Z) \xrightarrow{\simeq} \mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal E}(\mathbf 1,J^\vee\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Z).$$ The unit-input algebra is equivalent, as a commutative $D$-algebra, to $D//\varpi$. Under the point and unit identifications of the partial and full terms, the first map of Equation <a href="#eq:norm-triangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:norm-triangle">[eq:norm-triangle]</a> for $J^\vee\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Z$ is multiplication by $(p(p-1))_p$ on $\mathcal H(Z)$.
  - Explanation: The coefficient algebra of the layer is the derived quotient of the ultraproduct ring by the prime, and external multiplication with ordinary coefficients is an equivalence.
- **claim-odd-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-odd-vanishing)): For every ordinary sequence $Z$ and every sequence of odd integers $r=(r_p)_p$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{map}}_{\mathcal E}(\mathbf 1,T^rJ^\vee\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Z)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Odd twists of the dual sphere object have vanishing mapping spectrum from the unit in the localized category.
- **claim-euler-layer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-comparison)): For every sequence of graded commutative algebras $X$, there are natural product-compatible equivalences $$\label{eq:layer} B(X)//t\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Phi(X) \xleftarrow{\simeq}Q(X)\otimes_D(D//\varpi).$$ The right-hand comparison applies to arbitrary spectrum inputs as well. The scalar action in the $\delta$ direction agrees with ordinary evaluation on $Q(X)$ followed by reduction. The comparisons are compatible with algebra units and maps of inputs. The first map uses the tautological Euler null on $S(2V)$, restricted to $S(V)$.
  - Explanation: The Euler quotient of the mapping algebra is naturally identified, multiplicatively, with the layer algebra and with ordinary reduction of the ordinary mapping algebra by the prime.
- **claim-first-koszul** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-first-koszul)): For constant input $\mathbf 1$, the comparison identifies $$B(\mathbf 1)//t\simeq F^*h_\Omega(Y_1)$$ as graded algebras. For $M=(\mathrm{MU}_{(p)})_p$ there is a compatible identification $$B(M)//t\simeq F^*h_\Omega(M\otimes Y_1).$$ These identifications preserve the algebra maps induced by the unit and the scalar action in the $\delta$ direction.
  - Explanation: The first Euler layer is identified as graded algebras with the pulled-back mapping algebra of the first Koszul object, also after tensoring with complex bordism.
- **claim-euler-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-layer-completion)): For a rational graded commutative algebra $C$ with a homogeneous chain-degree-zero polynomial class $t$, define $$\widehat C=\lim_{s\geq1}C//t^s,$$ using the natural derived polynomial quotient maps. For every $r\geq1$, the natural map induces an equivalence $$\widehat C//t^r\simeq C//t^r.$$ Thus $\widehat C$ is complete for derived reduction by powers of $t$. Any algebra extension whose underlying $\widehat C$-module is perfect is complete as well. In particular these statements apply to $\widehat B(X)=\lim_s B(X)//t^s$.
  - Explanation: Derived completion at a degree-zero polynomial class preserves finite derived quotients, is complete, and extends to perfect algebra extensions.
- **claim-triangular** (theorem, [source](#stmt-universal-triangular)): Definition <a href="#universal:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="universal:coefficients">25</a> determines dg algebras $R^a$, $K_-^a$, and $K^a$. The differential of each $z_{j,S}$ is decomposable and uses only earlier generators when the generators are ordered by increasing $j$ and, for a fixed $j$, by decreasing binary order on subsets of $[j)$. There is a dg algebra evaluation $$\label{universal:evaluation} R^a\longrightarrow A\|_\Lambda=h_\Lambda(\mathbf 1)$$ that sends $z_{j,S}$ to the coefficient of $e_S$ in $b_j$. The constructions and evaluations extend those at every earlier stage.
  - Explanation: The universal coefficient algebras are differential graded with a triangular decomposable differential and admit an evaluation to the actual coefficient algebra compatible across stages.
- **claim-filtered-cells** (theorem, [source](#stmt-universal-filtered-cells)): The length filtrations are dg filtrations. The associated graded algebra $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}R^a$ has zero differential, and in $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}K^a$ one has $$\partial e_j=z_{j,\emptyset}.$$ As a filtered $R^a$-module, $K^a$ is built by finitely many free cells indexed by the subsets $S\subseteq\{0,\ldots,a\}$, in increasing binary order. The cell $e_S$ has chain degree $\|S\|$, weight $\sum_{h\in S}w_h$, and filtration order $\|S\|$. In particular, $K^a$ and $K_-^a$ are perfect $R^a$-modules.
  - Explanation: The length filtration is a differential graded filtration whose associated graded has zero differential on coefficients, exhibiting the Koszul algebra as a finite free-cell perfect module.
- **claim-associated-graded** (theorem, [source](#stmt-universal-associated-graded)): On filtered spectra, the functor $$(\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\mathcal F)_s =\mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}(\mathcal F_{s+1}\longrightarrow\mathcal F_s)$$ preserves colimits and is symmetric monoidal. It therefore preserves free commutative algebras and algebra pushouts. The comparison $$h_\Lambda(\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}X_p)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}h_\Lambda(X_p)$$ respects the algebra and module pairings whenever they are present.
  - Explanation: The associated graded functor on filtered spectra preserves colimits and the monoidal structure, hence free algebras and pushouts, and is compatible with the mapping functors.
- **claim-primewise-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-universal-primewise-models)): For the chosen cycles through $b_a$, there exist the following data as germs along $\mathcal U$:* 1. *Filtered $\Lambda$-graded commutative ring spectra $R_p^a$, with an equivalence $R^a\simeq h_\Lambda(R_p^a)$ at every filtration order, compatible with the filtered algebra structures.* 2. *Maps of whole unfiltered graded commutative algebras $R_p^a\to S_{(p)}$, where the sphere is constant in the weight grading, together with a homotopy identifying their normalized evaluation with Equation <a href="#universal:evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="universal:evaluation">[universal:evaluation]</a>.* 3. *Filtered finite-cell $R_p^a$-modules $K_p^a$, with an equivalence $K^a\simeq h_\Lambda(K_p^a)$ of filtered modules.* *The algebra models, their comparisons, and the evaluation homotopies may be chosen relative to the preceding stage. Thus the maps $R^{a-1}\to R^a$ and $R_p^{a-1}\to R_p^a$ and their evaluations form compatible diagrams of algebras. No algebra structure is required on $K_p^a$.
  - Explanation: Primewise filtered commutative ring models of the universal algebras, evaluation maps to the sphere and finite-cell module models exist as germs along the ultrafilter, compatibly across stages.
- **claim-whole-module** (theorem, [source](#stmt-universal-whole-module)): On one $\mathcal U$-large set of primes there is an equivalence of whole $(\text{weight},\text{order})$-graded $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a$-modules $$\label{eq:whole-module} \mathop{\mathrm{gr}}K_p^a\simeq \bigotimes_{j\leq a}^{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a} \mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}\bigl(\text{multiplication by }z_{j,\emptyset} \text{ on }\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a\bigr).$$ Each multiplication map has source the free module whose generator has weight $w_j$, order one, and ordinary degree $d_j$. On the right, $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a$ is the free graded algebra of all the generator spheres, with the splittings constructed in Proposition <a href="#universal:primewise-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="universal:primewise-models">29</a>.
  - Explanation: On one large set of primes the associated graded Koszul module is a tensor of two-term cofibers on the empty-subset generators, as whole graded modules.
- **claim-stable-elements** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-stable-elements)): Let $p$ be odd, let $0\leq k<p^2$, and write $k=lp+b$ with $0\leq b<p$. Let $H=(C_p)^l\subseteq\Sigma_k$ act by translations on $l$ disjoint blocks of $p$ letters, and let $N$ be its normalizer. For either the trivial or the sign module $M$ over $\mathbb F_p$, restriction identifies $$H^*(\Sigma_k;M) \xrightarrow{\ \cong\ } H^*(H;M)^{N/H}.$$ Here the normalizer acts both by conjugation on $H$ and by its given action on $M$. Moreover, $$N/H\cong \big((\mathbb F_p^\times)^l\rtimes\Sigma_l\big)\times\Sigma_b.$$
  - Explanation: Below the square of the prime, cohomology of symmetric groups with trivial or sign coefficients is computed by invariants of an elementary abelian Sylow subgroup under its normalizer quotient.
- **claim-symmetric-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-conn-symmetric)): Fix a positive integer $C$. For every odd prime $p>C$, every $0\leq k\leq Cp$, and every integer $d$, one has $$\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^k(S^d)\in\mathrm{Sp}_{\geq kd}.$$ If $d$ is odd, there is the additional bound $$\label{conn:odd-bound} \mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^k(S^d)\in\mathrm{Sp}_{\geq kd+k-(2C+1)}.$$ If $d$ is even, the objects in Equation <a href="#conn:cofiber-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="conn:cofiber-definition">[conn:cofiber-definition]</a> satisfy $$\label{conn:cofiber-bound} T_{p,k}(d)\in\mathrm{Sp}_{\geq kd+2k-3C}.$$ More precisely, writing $k=lp+b$ with $0\leq b<p$, an odd-degree extended power is contractible if $b\geq2$, and otherwise its cohomology above its rank begins no earlier than $l(p-2)$. For even $d$ and $k>0$, the multiplication cofiber is contractible if $b>0$, and if $b=0$ its cohomology above its rank begins no earlier than $l(2p-3)$.
  - Explanation: Connectivity bounds for symmetric powers of spheres and multiplication cofibers, with improvements for odd and even sphere degrees, controlled by the cohomology of the symmetric groups.
- **claim-koszul-connectivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-conn-estimate)): Fix $0\leq a<n$ and $\lambda,\delta\in\Lambda$, and set $$\label{conn:constants} C=\max\{0,\sigma(\lambda)\}+\|\sigma(\delta)\|+1, \qquad N_a=2^{a+1}-1, \qquad E=N_a(5C+1).$$ On a set of primes in $\mathcal U$, and for all its sufficiently large members, the underlying unfiltered module satisfies $$\label{conn:module-bound} K_p^a(p\lambda+\delta) \in\mathrm{Sp}_{\geq d(p\lambda+\delta) +2\nu(p\lambda+\delta)-E}.$$ The constants are independent of the individual multiplicities occurring at this weight and of the prime.
  - Explanation: The universal Koszul module at the moving weight has a lower connectivity bound with linear gain in the weight and a loss independent of the prime and of multiplicities.
- **claim-degree-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-degree-gap)): For every fixed $i=(\lambda,\delta,m)$ with $m>-\nu(\lambda)$, one has $$Q(K_p^a)_i=0,\qquad \Phi(K_p^a)_i=0.$$ Here $Q$ and $\Phi$ are applied to the primewise underlying graded modules, as allowed by the arbitrary-object part of the layer comparison.
  - Explanation: For fixed indices beyond the boundary row, both mapping objects of the primewise Koszul module vanish because the degree gap diverges with the prime.
- **claim-k-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-k-test)): For every $\lambda\in\Lambda$ and every integer $m>-\nu(\lambda)$, there is a vanishing of $\delta$-graded modules $$\label{eq:k-test} K^a\otimes_{R^a} \big(B(R_p^a)/t\big)_{\lambda,*,m}=0.$$ The tensor product uses the $R^a$-action in the $\delta$-grading. The notation $/t$ denotes the underlying module cofiber of multiplication by $t$.
  - Explanation: After tensoring with the Koszul algebra, the Euler quotient of the mapping algebra vanishes in rows past the boundary.
- **claim-amitsur** (theorem, [source](#stmt-norm-amitsur)): Let $R$ be a commutative graded rational algebra, and let $K$ be a commutative $R$-algebra whose underlying $R$-module is perfect. Define $$\mathcal T(U)= \mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}\bigl(K^{\otimes_R(\bullet+1)}\otimes_R U\bigr), \qquad c_U:U\longrightarrow\mathcal T(U),$$ using the Amitsur coaugmentation. Then $\mathcal T$ is exact, $\mathcal T(U)$ is $K$-local, and $c_U$ becomes an equivalence after tensoring with $K$. Consequently $c_U$ is an equivalence whenever $U$ is $K$-local, and $\mathcal T$ sends every $K$-acyclic module to zero. On commutative $R$-algebras the construction and its coaugmentation are maps of commutative algebras.
  - Explanation: Amitsur totalization over a perfect algebra is exact, produces local objects, is an equivalence on local inputs, kills acyclic ones and preserves commutative algebra structure.
- **claim-target-locality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-norm-target-locality)): Every $(\lambda,m)$ row of $L_a$ is $K^a$-local for the evaluated $R^a$-action. Thus the coaugmentation $c_{L_a}:L_a\to\mathcal T_a L_a$ is an equivalence of $I$-graded algebras.
  - Explanation: The completed target algebra is local for the Koszul algebra row by row, so its Amitsur coaugmentation is an equivalence of graded algebras.
- **claim-boundary-row** (theorem, [source](#stmt-norm-normalization)): For $B=B(R_p^a)$, the map $$s_{\mathcal T_a B}: (\mathcal T_a B)^\#\longrightarrow \bigl((\mathcal T_a B)[t^{-1}]\bigr)^\flat$$ is an equivalence of $\Lambda$-graded rational algebras.
  - Explanation: On the completed algebra, multiplication by the Euler parameter is an equivalence past the boundary row, so the regrading map into the inverted algebra is an equivalence.
- **claim-recovery-naturality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-norm-naturality)): The normalized map recovers the evaluated power after inversion: $$\label{norm:raw-recovery} s_{L_a}P^a\simeq (v[t^{-1}])^\flat P_{R_p^a}.$$ For $a>0$, its restriction to $R^{a-1}$ agrees, as a rational algebra map, with $P^{a-1}$ followed by the map $L_{a-1}^\#\to L_a^\#$.
  - Explanation: The normalized power recovers the evaluated unnormalized power after inversion and is compatible with restriction to the previous stage.
- **claim-attachment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-norm-attachment)): The preceding choices extend Equation <a href="#eq:target-layer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:target-layer">[eq:target-layer]</a> to stage $a+1$, with its unit, initial-layer, and tower compatibilities. Both new algebras are derived $t$-complete.
  - Explanation: Attaching the next null extends the layer equivalences, their unit and tower compatibilities, and preserves completeness.
- **claim-towers** (construction, [source](#stmt-norm-construction)): There exist cycles $b_0,\ldots,b_n$ and their realizations $Y_0,\ldots,Y_{n+1}$, together with the algebras $L_a,L_a^M$ for $0\leq a\leq n$, satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:target-layer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:target-layer">[eq:target-layer]</a> and its sphere-to-$M$, initial-layer, tower-map, and ordinary $\delta$-scalar compatibilities. Each $b_j$ has chain degree zero and weight $w_j$. For each $0\leq a<n$ there are rational algebra maps $P^a$ and $f^a$ as constructed above, and $g_a=f^a(b_a^u)$ has chain degree zero and weight $i_a$. It defines the next tower attachment and reduces modulo $t$ to $b_{a+1}$ under Equation <a href="#eq:target-layer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:target-layer">[eq:target-layer]</a>.
  - Explanation: The successive attaching cycles, their realizations and the auxiliary algebras are constructed inductively with all compatibilities, and each new cycle reduces to the next one.
- **claim-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-norm-recovery)): For $0\leq a<n$ there is a map of $\Lambda$-graded rational algebras $$\label{eq:recovery} h_\Lambda(M\otimes Y_a) \simeq h_\Lambda(M)\otimes_{R^a}K_-^a \longrightarrow \bigl(L_a^M[t^{-1}]\bigr)^\flat.$$ On $h_\Lambda(M)$ it is the unnormalized power $P_M$ followed by the map to the completed tower and inversion. The $M$-Hurewicz image of $b_a$ maps to $t g_a^M$, where $g_a^M$ is the image of $g_a$ in $L_a^M$.
  - Explanation: The unnormalized power on complex bordism extends over the preceding Koszul object, and the bordism Hurewicz image of the current cycle maps to the Euler parameter times the image of the attaching class.
- **claim-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cob-regularity)): The sequence $x_0,x_1,\ldots$ is regular in $(M_p)_*$. Under the multiplicative projection to $\mathrm{BP}_*$, the ideal $I_h$ has image $(p,v_1,\ldots,v_{h-1})$ for $h\geq 1$, and the image of $x_h$ modulo this ideal is a scalar unit times $v_h$. Moreover, $$k_u\in I_h\qquad(1\leq u<p^h).$$
  - Explanation: The coefficients of the series of multiplication by the prime form a regular sequence in cobordism coefficients, projecting to the Hazewinkel generators up to units.
- **claim-borel-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cob-cohomology)): For the Euler coordinate $x$ of a faithful complex character of $C_p$, restriction gives $$\label{cob:borel-rings} H_{C_p}^*=(M_p)^*(\mathrm{pt})[[x]]/([p](x)), \qquad H_G^*\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ } (H_{C_p}^*)^{\mathbb F_p^\times}.$$ The projective-space formulas are $$M_p^*(BK\times\mathbb CP^r)=H_K^*[Z]/(Z^{r+1}), \qquad M_p^*(BK\times\mathbb CP^\infty)=H_K^*[[Z]],$$ with graded power series in the infinite-dimensional case. Evaluation at a point of $BG$ detects units in $H_G^0$.
  - Explanation: Borel cobordism cohomology of the cyclic group and of the affine group is computed from the prime series, with the usual projective-space formulas.
- **claim-euler-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cob-euler-divisibility)): If a homogeneous class $y\in H_G^*$ restricts to an element of $(x^p)\subset H_{C_p}^*$, then $y$ is divisible by $\epsilon^2$ in $H_G^*$.
  - Explanation: A class on the affine group whose restriction lies deep in the Euler filtration is divisible by the square of the Euler class.
- **claim-power-rules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cob-power-rules)): The operation $\Pi_p$ is natural in $X$. For homogeneous even-degree classes $f$ and $g$, its product rule has the form $$\Pi_p(fg)=\alpha\,\Pi_p(f)\Pi_p(g), \qquad \alpha\in(H_G^0)^\times,$$ where $\alpha$ depends on their degrees and the Thom choices and is pulled back from $BG$. On any finite sum of classes in the same even degree, $\Pi_p$ is additive modulo $\mathfrak t_G(X)$. For $X=\mathbb CP^r$, extracting a coefficient of $Z^s$ sends $\mathfrak t_G(X)$ into $\mathfrak t_G$.
  - Explanation: The Thom-corrected power operation is natural, multiplicative up to units pulled back from the classifying space, and additive modulo transfers from groups of order prime to the prime.
- **claim-euler-power** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cob-euler-power)): There is a unit $U(Z)\in M_p^0(BG\times\mathbb CP^\infty)^\times$ such that $$\label{cob:euler-product} h(Z)=U(Z)\,e_{M_p}\bigl(P\otimes (\rho\otimes_{\mathbb R}\mathbb C)\bigr).$$ In particular, $h(Z)$ has no constant term and $$\label{cob:linear-coefficient} [Z]h(Z)=U(0)\epsilon, \qquad h(Z)\|_{C_p}=U(Z)\|_{C_p} \prod_{\alpha=0}^{p-1}\bigl(Z+_{\mathrm{MU}}[\alpha](x)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The power of the universal Euler class is a unit times the Euler class of a tensor bundle, with no constant term and an explicit linear coefficient and restriction to the cyclic group.
- **claim-coefficient-relation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cob-coefficient-relation)): For each integer $a\geq 0$ there are homogeneous coefficients in $H_G^*$ and units $C,C'\in(H_G^0)^\times$ such that $$\label{eq:cobordism} \begin{split} C\Pi_p(x_a)={}&C'\epsilon x_{a+1}+\epsilon^2D_* +\sum_{0\leq j,k\leq a}D_{jk}x_jx_k\\ &+\sum_{0\leq j<a}H_j\Pi_p(x_j)+T_*, \qquad T_*\in\mathfrak t_G. \end{split}$$ All terms have cohomological degree $-p d_a$; coefficients in the two sums and $D_*$ are in the complementary degrees.
  - Explanation: A relation in Borel cobordism expresses the power of one series coefficient as a unit times the Euler class times the next coefficient plus errors that are square multiples of the Euler class, products of earlier coefficients, earlier powers and transfers.
- **claim-cofiber-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-cofiber-quotient)): Let $E$ be a ring spectrum whose coefficient ring $R=\pi_*E$ is graded commutative and concentrated in even degrees. Let $J$ be a homogeneous ideal of $R$, and let $u:S\to Y$ induce the quotient identification $E_*Y\cong R/J$. Suppose that $b:S^d\to Y$ and $\mu:\Sigma^dY\to Y$, with $d$ even, satisfy $\mu\circ\Sigma^du\simeq b$. If the $E$-Hurewicz image of $b$ is $a$ times the image of a homogeneous element $x\in R_d$, where $a\in R_0^\times$ and multiplication by $x$ is injective on $R/J$, then the composite $S\xrightarrow{u}Y\to\mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}(\mu)$ induces $$E_*\mathop{\mathrm{cofib}}(\mu)\cong R/(J,x).$$ This is an identification of the entire graded module.
  - Explanation: Coning off a map whose homology image is a unit times a regular element computes the expected quotient module of homology in all degrees.
- **claim-t-injectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-t-injectivity)): Suppose that, on a $\mathcal U$-large set of primes, $$(M_p)_*Y_{a+1,p}\cong (M_p)_*/I_{a+1}$$ via the unit. Then multiplication by $t$ is injective on $\pi_0L_a^M$ at every index. In particular, $$\pi_0(L_a^M)_i\longrightarrow \pi_0(L_a^M[t^{-1}])_i$$ is injective for every $i\in I$.
  - Explanation: Under the quotient hypothesis on the preceding object, multiplication by the Euler parameter is injective on degree-zero homotopy of the bordism auxiliary algebra, so it injects into the inverted algebra.
- **claim-raw-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-raw-powers)): Assume the hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#real:t-injectivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="real:t-injectivity">50</a>. Suppose also that $(M_p)_*Y_{a,p}\cong (M_p)_*/I_a$ via the unit on a $\mathcal U$-large set, and that the $M$-Hurewicz image of $b_a$ equals $u x_a$ for some $u\in D^\times$. Then, before inverting $t$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal P(x_j)&=0 &&\text{in }\pi_0(L_a^M)_{s_j}\quad(0\leq j<a),\\ t g_a&=\mathcal P(u)\mathcal P(x_a) &&\text{in }\pi_0(L_a^M)_{s_a}. \end{aligned} \label{eq:before-inversion}$$ Here $g_a$ also denotes its image under base change to $M$, and $\mathcal P(u)$ is a unit at index zero.
  - Explanation: Before inversion, raw powers of earlier coefficients vanish and the Euler parameter times the attaching class equals the product of unit and raw power of the current coefficient.
- **claim-thom-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-thom-comparisons)): Fix $0\leq a<n$, and suppose that $(M_p)_*Y_{a+1,p}\cong(M_p)_*/I_{a+1}$ via the unit on a $\mathcal U$-large set. The maps in Equation <a href="#real:borel-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="real:borel-comparison">[real:borel-comparison]</a> have the following properties.* 1. *The image $\theta_\tau(\epsilon)$ is a unit at index zero times $t$.* 2. *For $j\leq a$, the image $\theta_{s_j}(\Pi_p(x_j))$ is a unit at index zero times $\mathcal P(x_j)$.* 3. *Put $\delta_j=(0,w_j,0)$. For $j\leq a$, the class $\theta_{\delta_j}(x_j)$ lies in the image of $$t:\pi_0(L_a^M)_{\delta_j-\tau} \longrightarrow\pi_0(L_a^M)_{\delta_j}.$$* 4. *Set $X_{a+1}=\theta_{i_a}(x_{a+1})$. Under Equation <a href="#real:target-layer-M" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="real:target-layer-M">[real:target-layer-M]</a>, its reduction modulo $t$ is a unit at index zero times the image of the ordinary coefficient $x_{a+1}$ in $(M_p)_*Y_{a+1,p}$, at weight $w_{a+1}$.* 5. *Every sequence of finite sums of additive transfers from prime-to-$p$ subgroups, in the degree assigned to any fixed index, has zero image under $\theta_i$. The number of summands may depend on $p$.
  - Explanation: Thom comparisons from Borel cohomology into the layer algebra send the Euler class to a unit times the Euler parameter, permutation powers to raw powers, and transfer errors to zero.
- **claim-hurewicz** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-hurewicz)): There is a $\mathcal U$-large set of primes on which the unit maps induce, simultaneously for $0\leq j\leq n+1$, $$(M_p)_*Y_{j,p}\cong (M_p)_*/I_j, \qquad \mathrm{BP}_*Y_{j,p}\cong\mathrm{BP}_*/\overline I_j. \label{real:homology-quotients}$$ For every $0\leq j\leq n$, there is a scalar $u^{(j)}\in D^\times$ such that the $M$-Hurewicz image of $b_j$ equals $u^{(j)}x_j$ in $\pi_0h_\Omega(M\otimes Y_j)_{w_j}$.
  - Explanation: On one large set of primes the successive cofibers have the expected bordism and Brown–Peterson homology quotients, and each attaching class has Hurewicz image a scalar unit times the corresponding series coefficient.
- **claim-finite-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-finite-descent)): The $p$-localizations of finite CW spectra are closed under the finite suspensions and cofibers in Equation <a href="#real:primewise-cofibers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="real:primewise-cofibers">[real:primewise-cofibers]</a>.
  - Explanation: Local finite CW spectra are closed under the finite suspensions and cofibers occurring in the primewise diagram, so the final spectrum is finite and local.

          "exact_quote": "For graded inputs we use an ultraproduct of categories of *whole* primewise graded systems. Evaluation at any fixed index gives a functor into $\\mathcal C$. Primewise graded pairings then give the diagrams and pairings used in Lemma <a href=\"#found:mapping-pairings\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"found:mapping-pairings\">7</a>, compatibly with restriction to large sets. One may first pull these systems back along prime-dependent additive maps from a common indexing lattice. This only uses a functor from the ultraproduct of whole systems to systems in $\\mathcal C$; it does not identify these categories or assert that every diagram in $\\mathcal C$ has such a lift. The same distinction applies to group actions, filtrations, and module structures. The simultaneous coherent sphere grading was chosen only in $\\mathcal C$. An individual sphere or cell shift used primewise is simply the sphere of the indicated degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-308.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-found-finite-cell-morita",
        "exact_quote": "The functor $h_\\Omega$ induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence between $\\mathcal C_{\\mathrm{cell}}$ and the category of finite-cell graded $A$-modules. For $X,Y\\in\\mathcal C$, the natural comparison $$\\label{eq:found-tensor-comparison}\n h_\\Omega(X)\\otimes_A h_\\Omega(Y)\n       \\longrightarrow h_\\Omega(X\\otimes Y)$$ is an equivalence whenever either input is in $\\mathcal C_{\\mathrm{cell}}$. These comparisons preserve units, compositions, and symmetric multi-input products. In particular, a commutative $A$-algebra whose underlying module is finite-cell determines a commutative algebra in $\\mathcal C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-found-koszul-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0\\leq r\\leq n$. Suppose cycles $b_h$ of chain degree zero and weight $w_h$, for $0\\leq h\\leq r$, have been chosen successively in the strict algebras $$\\label{eq:found-exterior-tower}\n \\begin{gathered}\n A[e_0,\\ldots,e_{j-1}],\\qquad\n |e_h|_{\\mathrm{chain}}=1,\\quad \\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(e_h)=w_h,\\\\\n \\partial e_h=b_h\\in A[e_0,\\ldots,e_{h-1}],\n \\qquad b_0=\\varpi u_0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ For $0\\leq j\\leq r+1$, these algebras determine commutative algebras $Y_j\\in\\mathcal C$, with $Y_0=\\mathbf 1$. For $0\\leq j\\leq r$, the cycle $b_j$ represents a map $S(w_j)\\to Y_j$, and there is a cofiber sequence $$\\label{eq:found-cofiber-tower}\n S(w_j)\\otimes Y_j\\xrightarrow{\\,b_j\\cdot-\\,}Y_j\n                    \\longrightarrow Y_{j+1},$$ whose second map is the algebra map adjoining the null $e_j$. For $M=[(\\mathrm{MU}_{(p)})_p]$ and $0\\leq j\\leq r+1$, there are compatible equivalences $$\\label{eq:found-mu-base-change}\n h_\\Omega(M\\otimes Y_j)\n   \\simeq h_\\Omega(M)\\otimes_A A[e_0,\\ldots,e_{j-1}].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-power-localizations",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal E$ be the symmetric monoidal stable localization of $\\mathrm{Ind}(\\mathcal C_G)$ by the localizing tensor ideal generated by $G_+\\otimes X$, for all $X\\in\\mathcal C_G$. For $b\\geq1$, put $$E_b=G^{*b},\\qquad\n \\widetilde E_b=\\mathop{\\mathrm{cofib}}(E_{b+}\\longrightarrow\\mathbf 1).$$ The image of every object of $\\mathcal C_G$ is compact in $\\mathcal E$, and, for $X,Y\\in\\mathcal C_G$, $$\\label{power:join-formula}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{map}}_{\\mathcal E}(X,Y)\n \\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_b\\mathop{\\mathrm{map}}_{\\mathcal C_G}(X,\\widetilde E_b\\otimes Y).$$ There is a further symmetric monoidal localization $\\mathcal E'$ in which $a_2$ is invertible. Its localization of an object $Y$ is represented in $\\mathcal E$ by $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{s\\geq0}\n \\bigl(Y\\xrightarrow{a_2}T^2Y\\xrightarrow{a_2}T^4Y\n \\longrightarrow\\cdots\\bigr).$$ Mapping out of a sequence object commutes with this telescope.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-power-localizations",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal E$ be the symmetric monoidal stable localization of $\\mathrm{Ind}(\\mathcal C_G)$ by the localizing tensor ideal generated by $G_+\\otimes X$, for all $X\\in\\mathcal C_G$. Its localization of an object $Y$ is represented in $\\mathcal E$ by $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{s\\geq0}\n \\bigl(Y\\xrightarrow{a_2}T^2Y\\xrightarrow{a_2}T^4Y\n \\longrightarrow\\cdots\\bigr).$$ Mapping out of a sequence object commutes with this telescope.",

        "anchor": "stmt-layer-even-trivializations",
        "exact_quote": "For every even integer $r$, including negative integers, the local $p$-local sphere of $rV$ over $Z_p^{\\mathrm{orb}}$ is trivializable with fiber degree $r(p-1)$.         "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-layer-even-trivializations",
          "exact_quote": "For every even integer $r$, including negative integers, the local $p$-local sphere of $rV$ over $Z_p^{\\mathrm{orb}}$ is trivializable with fiber degree $r(p-1)$.         "anchor": "stmt-universal-filtered-cells",
        "exact_quote": "The length filtrations are dg filtrations. The associated graded algebra $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}R^a$ has zero differential, and in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}K^a$ one has $$\\partial e_j=z_{j,\\emptyset}.$$ As a filtered $R^a$-module, $K^a$ is built by finitely many free cells indexed by the subsets $S\\subseteq\\{0,\\ldots,a\\}$, in increasing binary order. The cell $e_S$ has chain degree $|S|$, weight $\\sum_{h\\in S}w_h$, and filtration order $|S|$. In particular, $K^a$ and $K_-^a$ are perfect $R^a$-modules.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"

      "exact_statement": "On filtered spectra, the functor $$(\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}\\mathcal F)_s\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{cofib}}(\\mathcal F_{s+1}\\longrightarrow\\mathcal F_s)$$ preserves colimits and is symmetric monoidal. The comparison $$h_\\Lambda(\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}X_p)\\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}h_\\Lambda(X_p)$$ respects the algebra and module pairings whenever they are present.",
      "explanation": "The associated graded functor on filtered spectra preserves colimits and the monoidal structure, hence free algebras and pushouts, and is compatible with the mapping functors.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-universal-primewise-models",
        "exact_quote": "For the chosen cycles through $b_a$, there exist the following data as germs along $\\mathcal U$:*\n\n1.  *Filtered $\\Lambda$-graded commutative ring spectra $R_p^a$, with an equivalence $R^a\\simeq h_\\Lambda(R_p^a)$ at every filtration order, compatible with the filtered algebra structures.*\n\n2.  *Maps of whole unfiltered graded commutative algebras $R_p^a\\to S_{(p)}$, where the sphere is constant in the weight grading, together with a homotopy identifying their normalized evaluation with Equation <a href=\"#universal:evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"universal:evaluation\">[universal:evaluation]</a>.*\n\n3.  *Filtered finite-cell $R_p^a$-modules $K_p^a$, with an equivalence $K^a\\simeq h_\\Lambda(K_p^a)$ of filtered modules.*\n\n*The algebra models, their comparisons, and the evaluation homotopies may be chosen relative to the preceding stage. Thus the maps $R^{a-1}\\to R^a$ and $R_p^{a-1}\\to R_p^a$ and their evaluations form compatible diagrams of algebras. No algebra structure is required on $K_p^a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-universal-whole-module",
        "exact_quote": "On one $\\mathcal U$-large set of primes there is an equivalence of whole $(\\text{weight},\\text{order})$-graded $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a$-modules $$\\label{eq:whole-module}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}K_p^a\\simeq\n \\bigotimes_{j\\leq a}^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{cofib}}\\bigl(\\text{multiplication by }z_{j,\\emptyset}\n                         \\text{ on }\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a\\bigr).$$ Each multiplication map has source the free module whose generator has weight $w_j$, order one, and ordinary degree $d_j$. On the right, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}R_p^a$ is the free graded algebra of all the generator spheres, with the splittings constructed in Proposition <a href=\"#universal:primewise-models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"universal:primewise-models\">29</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-conn-stable-elements",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be odd, let $0\\leq k<p^2$, and write $k=lp+b$ with $0\\leq b<p$. Let $H=(C_p)^l\\subseteq\\Sigma_k$ act by translations on $l$ disjoint blocks of $p$ letters, and let $N$ be its normalizer. For either the trivial or the sign module $M$ over $\\mathbb F_p$, restriction identifies $$H^*(\\Sigma_k;M)\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\cong\\ }\n H^*(H;M)^{N/H}.$$ Here the normalizer acts both by conjugation on $H$ and by its given action on $M$. Moreover, $$N/H\\cong\n \\big((\\mathbb F_p^\\times)^l\\rtimes\\Sigma_l\\big)\\times\\Sigma_b.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-conn-symmetric",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive integer $C$. For every odd prime $p>C$, every $0\\leq k\\leq Cp$, and every integer $d$, one has $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^k(S^d)\\in\\mathrm{Sp}_{\\geq kd}.$$ If $d$ is odd, there is the additional bound $$\\label{conn:odd-bound}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}^k(S^d)\\in\\mathrm{Sp}_{\\geq kd+k-(2C+1)}.$$ If $d$ is even, the objects in Equation <a href=\"#conn:cofiber-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:cofiber-definition\">[conn:cofiber-definition]</a> satisfy $$\\label{conn:cofiber-bound}\n T_{p,k}(d)\\in\\mathrm{Sp}_{\\geq kd+2k-3C}.$$ More precisely, writing $k=lp+b$ with $0\\leq b<p$, an odd-degree extended power is contractible if $b\\geq2$, and otherwise its cohomology above its rank begins no earlier than $l(p-2)$. For even $d$ and $k>0$, the multiplication cofiber is contractible if $b>0$, and if $b=0$ its cohomology above its rank begins no earlier than $l(2p-3)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-conn-symmetric",

For even $d$ and $k>0$, the multiplication cofiber is contractible if $b>0$, and if $b=0$ its cohomology above its rank begins no earlier than $l(2p-3)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-norm-amitsur",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a commutative graded rational algebra, and let $K$ be a commutative $R$-algebra whose underlying $R$-module is perfect. Define $$\\mathcal T(U)=\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tot}}\\bigl(K^{\\otimes_R(\\bullet+1)}\\otimes_R U\\bigr),\n \\qquad c_U:U\\longrightarrow\\mathcal T(U),$$ using the Amitsur coaugmentation. Then $\\mathcal T$ is exact, $\\mathcal T(U)$ is $K$-local, and $c_U$ becomes an equivalence after tensoring with $K$. Consequently $c_U$ is an equivalence whenever $U$ is $K$-local, and $\\mathcal T$ sends every $K$-acyclic module to zero. On commutative $R$-algebras the construction and its coaugmentation are maps of commutative algebras.",
        "source_id": "PM-308.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-norm-amitsur",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a commutative graded rational algebra, and let $K$ be a commutative $R$-algebra whose underlying $R$-module is perfect. Then $\\mathcal T$ is exact, $\\mathcal T(U)$ is $K$-local, and $c_U$ becomes an equivalence after tensoring with $K$. Consequently $c_U$ is an equivalence whenever $U$ is $K$-local, and $\\mathcal T$ sends every $K$-acyclic module to zero. On commutative $R$-algebras the construction and its coaugmentation are maps of commutative algebras.",
          "source_id": "PM-308.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $R$ be a commutative graded rational algebra, and let $K$ be a commutative $R$-algebra whose underlying $R$-module is perfect. Define $$\\mathcal T(U)=\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tot}}\\bigl(K^{\\otimes_R(\\bullet+1)}\\otimes_R U\\bigr),\n \\qquad c_U:U\\longrightarrow\\mathcal T(U),$$ using the Amitsur coaugmentation. Then $\\mathcal T$ is exact, $\\mathcal T(U)$ is $K$-local, and $c_U$ becomes an equivalence after tensoring with $K$.         "anchor": "stmt-real-cofiber-quotient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a ring spectrum whose coefficient ring $R=\\pi_*E$ is graded commutative and concentrated in even degrees. Let $J$ be a homogeneous ideal of $R$, and let $u:S\\to Y$ induce the quotient identification $E_*Y\\cong R/J$. Suppose that $b:S^d\\to Y$ and $\\mu:\\Sigma^dY\\to Y$, with $d$ even, satisfy $\\mu\\circ\\Sigma^du\\simeq b$. If the $E$-Hurewicz image of $b$ is $a$ times the image of a homogeneous element $x\\in R_d$, where $a\\in R_0^\\times$ and multiplication by $x$ is injective on $R/J$, then the composite $S\\xrightarrow{u}Y\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{cofib}}(\\mu)$ induces $$E_*\\mathop{\\mathrm{cofib}}(\\mu)\\cong R/(J,x).$$ This is an identification of the entire graded module.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Smith–Toda complex** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A Smith–Toda complex $V(n)$ is a finite $p$-local spectrum $X$ with $$\mathrm{BP}_*X\cong\mathrm{BP}_*/(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n)$$ as graded $\mathrm{BP}_*\mathrm{BP}$-comodules, with the quotient coaction and its generator in degree zero.
- **finite local spectrum** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here finite $p$-local means a retract of the $p$-localization of a finite CW spectrum.
- **large set of primes** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A set belonging to $\mathcal U$ will be called *large*.
- **finite-cell graded module** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A *finite-cell* graded $A$-module is obtained from finitely many homological suspensions of the modules $A\langle\eta\rangle$ by finite sums and cofibers.
- **acyclic module** ([source](#pm-h-28)): For a commutative graded algebra $R$ and a commutative $R$-algebra $K$, call an $R$-module $V$ *$K$-acyclic* if $K\otimes_R V=0$.
- **local module** ([source](#pm-h-28)): An $R$-module $U$ is *$K$-local* if $\mathop{\mathrm{map}}_R(V,U)=0$ for every $K$-acyclic $V$.
- **universal coefficient algebra** ([source](#stmt-universal-coefficients)): The graded algebra underlying $R^a$ is the free graded-commutative $D$-algebra on variables $$z_{j,S},\qquad 0\leq j\leq a,\quad S\subseteq[j), \qquad \|z_{j,S}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}=-\|S\|, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(z_{j,S})=\gamma_{j,S} :=w_j-\sum_{h\in S}w_h.$$ Set $$b_j^u=\sum_{S\subseteq[j)}z_{j,S}e_S, \qquad \partial e_j=b_j^u.$$ The differential on $R^a$ is determined by $\partial b_j^u=0$, with coefficients compared in the exterior basis. Define $$K_-^a=R^a[e_j:j<a],\qquad K^a=R^a[e_j:j\leq a].$$ The superscript $u$ indicates these universal cycles.

| q-bp-degree | degree of the Hazewinkel generator v_i | = | $2(p^i-1)$ | each index i |
| q-sphere-degree | sphere degree of the weight basis element | = | $2(p^h-1)$ | each basis element |
| q-first-step | first degree step q | = | $2(p-1)$ | odd prime p |
| q-valuation | valuation of each weight generator | = | $1$ | each generator |
| q-exactness-connectivity | lower connectivity bound of the Euler transformation mapping spectrum | ≥ | $p$ | odd prime p |
| q-group-order | order of the affine group | = | $p(p-1)$ | odd prime p |
| q-mass-bound | mass of a coefficient generator weight | ≥ | $\frac{3^j+1}{2}$ | each generator |
| q-cell-count | number of basis vectors of the Koszul module | = | $2^{a+1}$ | stage a |
| q-odd-power-bound | connectivity of an odd-degree symmetric power | ≥ | $kd+k-(2C+1)$ | odd d, odd prime above C, k up to a multiple of the prime |
| q-even-cofiber-bound | connectivity of the multiplication cofiber | ≥ | $kd+2k-3C$ | even d, odd prime above C |
| q-module-loss | connectivity loss E for the Koszul module | = | $N_a(5C+1)$ | stage a and fixed weights |
| q-generator-count | number of generator types N_a | = | $2^{a+1}-1$ | stage a |
| q-series-degree | degree of the series coefficient x_h | = | $2(p^h-1)$ | each h |
| q-euler-degree | cohomological degree of the Euler class | = | $2(p-1)$ | odd prime p |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-ultraproduct** [claim](#stmt-found-ultraproduct) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-ultraproduct): Simplicial presentations and filtered colimits of categories -> stable rational ultraproduct category
- **step-rectification** [claim](#stmt-found-rectification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-rectification): Rational comparison of operads and exact coinvariants -> strict algebra models
- **step-free-null** [claim](#stmt-found-free-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-free-null): Free disk algebra resolving the augmentation -> Koszul extension equals module cofiber
- **step-picard-grading** [claim](#stmt-found-picard-grading) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-picard-grading): Infinite loop space of the Picard spectrum and obstruction groups killed by two -> coherent sphere grading
- **step-mapping-pairings** [claim](#stmt-found-mapping-pairings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-mapping-pairings): Tensor of maps with the sphere grading -> graded mapping algebras
- **step-finite-cell-morita** [claim](#stmt-found-finite-cell-morita) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-finite-cell-morita): Generator check and exactness, tensor bar construction -> finite-cell equivalence
- **step-koszul-realization** [claim](#stmt-found-koszul-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-koszul-realization): Finite basis of exterior monomials with filtered differential -> cofiber tower of objects
- **step-localizations** [claim](#stmt-power-localizations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-power-localizations): Join filtration telescope and compactness -> two localizations
- **step-prime-to-p** [claim](#stmt-power-prime-to-p) / [proof](#proof-stmt-power-prime-to-p): Norm composite equals the group order, invertible locally -> retract of induced objects
- **step-exactness** [claim](#stmt-power-exactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-power-exactness): Cube indexed by subsets, connectivity of the Euler transformation space -> exact permutation power
- **step-euler-inversion** [claim](#stmt-power-graded-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-power-graded-localization): Telescope along Euler multiplication -> inverted mapping algebra
- **step-raw-power** [claim](#stmt-power-operation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-power-operation): Exact power followed by the multiplication map -> unnormalized power algebra map
- **step-acyclic-sphere** [claim](#stmt-layer-acyclic-sphere) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-acyclic-sphere): Cohomology of the cyclic group, multiplier invariants, stable Hurewicz -> orbit sphere acyclic
- **step-even-trivializations** [claim](#stmt-layer-even-trivializations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-even-trivializations): Postnikov induction on sphere automorphisms -> trivialized twisted spheres
- **step-norm-model** [claim](#stmt-layer-norm-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-norm-model): Group ring bimodule norm element -> partial and full norm triangle
- **step-partial-thom** [claim](#stmt-layer-partial-thom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-partial-thom): Cell counts and group homology with determinant twist -> partial Thom computation
- **step-layer-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-layer-coefficient-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-coefficient-algebra): Pairing of norm triangles and cofiber of multiplication by the group order -> coefficient algebra of the layer
- **step-odd-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-layer-odd-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-odd-vanishing): Norm equivalence by prime-to-p stabilizers -> odd twists vanish
- **step-euler-layer** [claim](#stmt-layer-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-comparison): Two comparisons through the layer algebra, restriction from the doubled sphere -> multiplicative Euler layer
- **step-first-koszul** [claim](#stmt-layer-first-koszul) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-first-koszul): Base change along the quotient of the ring by the prime -> first Koszul layer
- **step-euler-completion** [claim](#stmt-layer-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-layer-completion): Pro-zero tower of cones after derived reduction -> complete algebras
- **step-triangular** [claim](#stmt-universal-triangular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-universal-triangular): Binary order on subsets and square of an odd derivation -> universal dg algebras and evaluation
- **step-filtered-cells** [claim](#stmt-universal-filtered-cells) / [proof](#proof-stmt-universal-filtered-cells): Length filtration of differentials -> finite free-cell module
- **step-associated-graded** [claim](#stmt-universal-associated-graded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-universal-associated-graded): Pushout-product of filtration arrows on steps -> monoidal associated graded
- **step-primewise-models** [claim](#stmt-universal-primewise-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-universal-primewise-models): Inductive cell attachment with extended-power computation at large primes -> primewise ring and module models
- **step-whole-module** [claim](#stmt-universal-whole-module) / [proof](#proof-stmt-universal-whole-module): Full faithfulness on finite free cells and lifting of finitely many homotopies -> whole-module equivalence on one large set
- **step-stable-elements** [claim](#stmt-conn-stable-elements) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-stable-elements): Sylow subgroup, transfer and double coset formula -> symmetric group cohomology as invariants
- **step-symmetric-bounds** [claim](#stmt-conn-symmetric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-symmetric): Cyclic group cohomology weights, restriction kernel, stable Hurewicz -> connectivity bounds
- **step-koszul-connectivity** [claim](#stmt-conn-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-estimate): Factor-by-factor bounds on the tensor decomposition and filtration induction -> module connectivity bound
- **step-degree-gap** [claim](#stmt-conn-degree-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-degree-gap): Divergence of the degree gap with the prime -> vanishing mapping objects
- **step-k-test** [claim](#stmt-conn-k-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-k-test): Balanced pairing checked on free cells -> Koszul-module vanishing
- **step-amitsur** [claim](#stmt-norm-amitsur) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-amitsur): Split cosimplicial object and dualizability -> completion functor
- **step-target-locality** [claim](#stmt-norm-target-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-target-locality): Module structure over the Koszul algebra and completeness -> local completed target
- **step-boundary-row** [claim](#stmt-norm-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-normalization): Constant telescope past the boundary row -> regrading equivalence
- **step-recovery-naturality** [claim](#stmt-norm-naturality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-naturality): Naturality of regrading and coaugmentation -> normalized power
- **step-attachment** [claim](#stmt-norm-attachment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-attachment): Pushouts commute with reduction; two-term cofiber at each index -> extended layer equivalence
- **step-towers** [claim](#stmt-norm-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-construction): Induction over the finitely many stages -> compatible towers of cycles and realizations
- **step-recovery** [claim](#stmt-norm-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-recovery): Pushout of algebra maps over the universal coefficients -> cobordism recovery map
- **step-regularity** [claim](#stmt-cob-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cob-regularity): Hazewinkel recursion, triangular change of generators, characteristic-p additivity -> regular series coefficients
- **step-borel-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-cob-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cob-cohomology): Cellular collapse, sphere bundle sequence, invariants for the complement -> Borel cobordism rings
- **step-euler-divisibility** [claim](#stmt-cob-euler-divisibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cob-euler-divisibility): Multiplier characters on graded pieces of the Euler filtration -> divisibility by the square of the Euler class
- **step-power-rules** [claim](#stmt-cob-power-rules) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cob-power-rules): Biproduct distribution with nonconstant label tuples giving transfers -> product and sum rules
- **step-euler-power** [claim](#stmt-cob-euler-power) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cob-euler-power): Fiberwise Thom maps and Euler product over characters -> Euler product formula
- **step-coefficient-relation** [claim](#stmt-cob-coefficient-relation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cob-coefficient-relation): Coefficient extraction on a truncated projective space -> cobordism relation
- **step-cofiber-quotient** [claim](#stmt-real-cofiber-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-cofiber-quotient): Cofiber long exact sequence with injective multiplication -> homology of the cofiber as a quotient
- **step-t-injectivity** [claim](#stmt-real-t-injectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-t-injectivity): Evenness of the layer and exact sequence of the Euler cofiber -> injectivity of Euler multiplication
- **step-raw-powers** [claim](#stmt-real-raw-powers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-raw-powers): Apply the recovery map and cancel after localization -> raw power identities before inversion
- **step-thom-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-real-thom-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-thom-comparisons): Thom comparison maps, unit ratios and retract of free induction for transfers -> properties of Borel comparison maps
- **step-hurewicz** [claim](#stmt-real-hurewicz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-hurewicz): Induction on the stage using the cobordism relation and cancellation of one Euler factor -> Hurewicz images and homology quotients on one large set
- **step-finite-descent** [claim](#stmt-real-finite-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-finite-descent): Compactness of finite cell spectra and finite mapping cones -> closure of local finite spectra
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-finite-descent-2): Choose a prime in the common large set and read off finiteness and comodule structure -> finite Smith–Toda complex

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-norm-naturality",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-norm-naturality</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The first assertion says that the following square commutes: $$\\begin{array}{ccc}\n R^a&\\xrightarrow{\\ P^a\\ }&L_a^\\#\\\\[2pt]\n {\\scriptstyle P_{R_p^a}}\\downarrow&&\n       \\downarrow{\\scriptstyle s_{L_a}}\\\\[2pt]\n (B[t^{-1}])^\\flat&\\xrightarrow{\\ (v[t^{-1}])^\\flat\\ }&\n       (L_a[t^{-1}])^\\flat .\n\\end{array}$$ To verify it, naturality of $s$ for $c_{L_a}$ and $\\mathcal T_a v$, followed by naturality of the coaugmentation for $v$, gives $$\\begin{aligned}\n &s_{L_a}(c_{L_a}^\\#)^{-1}(\\mathcal T_a v)^\\#\n        s_{\\mathcal T_a B}^{-1}(c_B[t^{-1}])^\\flat\\\\\n &\\qquad=\\bigl((c_{L_a}[t^{-1}])^\\flat\\bigr)^{-1}\n       \\bigl((\\mathcal T_a v)[t^{-1}]\\bigr)^\\flat\n       (c_B[t^{-1}])^\\flat\n   \\simeq (v[t^{-1}])^\\flat.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The inverse in this display is the localization of an already invertible coaugmentation on $L_a$.",
          "source_id": "PM-308.1"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-prime-depends-on-height**: For each height n the theorem asserts existence of some prime p and a finite p-local spectrum realizing the quotient of Brown-Peterson homology by the first powers of p, v_1, ..., v_n; the prime may depend on n.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-fixed-prime**: Use the theorem to claim a V(n) exists at a predetermined prime for a general height. -> The result gives existence at some prime depending on n only; for a fixed large prime a nonexistence result obstructs a height depending on the prime.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence statement whose prime is selected through a nonprincipal ultrafilter on the odd primes; no finite procedure computes the prime or the spectrum, so there is no constructive algorithm to implement or test.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-prime-depends-on-height** (hypothesis): The existence of the Smith–Toda complex is asserted only with the prime chosen after the height; the source states it does not give one prime for all heights nor an explicit bound on the prime. Blocked use: ['Asserting that V(n) exists at a predetermined prime for a general height', 'Extracting an explicit prime from the construction']
- **limit-no-multiplication** (formal_scope): The theorem concerns an ordinary finite local spectrum with a prescribed Brown–Peterson comodule; the source states it does not require a multiplication on the final spectrum. Blocked use: ['Treating the constructed spectrum as a ring spectrum', 'Using it where an associative multiplication is needed']
- **limit-nonconstructive-ultrafilter** (nonconstructive): The ambient category is formed from a nonprincipal ultrafilter on the odd primes, and the final prime is only known to lie in a large set of primes, so no procedure produces it. Blocked use: ['Writing a program that outputs the prime or the spectrum for a given height', 'Claiming a running time for the construction']
- **limit-large-set-finite-diagram** (hypothesis): Descent to a single prime uses only a finite diagram of maps and homotopies on a common large set of primes; full commutative algebra structures on the objects Y_j are not descended. Blocked use: ['Assuming the primewise objects carry commutative algebra structures', 'Intersecting infinitely many large sets of primes']
- **limit-power-not-linear** (approximation): The unnormalized power operation is a map of algebras over rational spectra only; no linearity over the larger scalar ring is asserted. Blocked use: ['Treating the power map as linear over the ultraproduct ring']
- **limit-large-prime-thresholds** (edge_case): The connectivity bounds hold only for sufficiently large primes in the ultrafilter, with thresholds that may depend on the fixed lattice indices. Blocked use: ['Applying the connectivity estimate at small primes', 'Assuming a uniform prime threshold over all indices']
- **limit-symmetric-range** (hypothesis): The symmetric power connectivity bounds require a fixed constant C, an odd prime larger than C, and multiplicity at most a multiple of the prime. Blocked use: ['Using the bounds for multiplicities beyond a multiple of the prime', 'Using them at primes not exceeding C']
- **limit-p-local-finite** (formal_scope): Finite means a retract of the localization of a finite CW spectrum; the result is not about integral spectra. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to unlocalized finite complexes']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every nonnegative integer <i>n</i>, we construct a Smith–Toda complex $`V(n)`$ at some prime <i>p</i> depending on <i>n</i>. It is a finite <i>p</i>-local spectrum whose Brown–Peterson homology is $`\mathrm{BP}_*/(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n)`$ with its canonical comodule structure and generator in degree zero. Each listed generator is killed to its first power.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Smith–Toda realization problem asks whether the first chromatic parameters can be killed in a finite spectrum. Algebraically these parameters form a regular sequence in Brown–Peterson homology. Topologically, killing the next parameter requires an actual map of finite spectra that induces its multiplication on homology. Regularity computes the homology of the cofiber once such a map exists; it does not supply the map. At a prime $p$, write $$\mathrm{BP}_* = \mathbb Z_{(p)}[v_1,v_2,\ldots],
 \qquad |v_i|=2(p^i-1),$$ using Hazewinkel generators. A Smith–Toda complex $V(n)$ is a finite $p$-local spectrum $X$ with $$\mathrm{BP}_*X\cong\mathrm{BP}_*/(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n)$$ as graded $\mathrm{BP}_*\mathrm{BP}$-comodules, with the quotient coaction and its generator in degree zero. Here finite $p$-local means a retract of the $p$-localization of a finite CW spectrum. The question concerns first powers. Suitable higher powers can be realized at every finite height by the periodicity methods of Hopkins–Smith (Hopkins and Smith 1998, Theorem 9 and Proposition 5.14) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[HopkinsSmith1998](#ref-HopkinsSmith1998)]. Those exponents are chosen as part of the construction; the question here prescribes every exponent to be one.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $n\geq0$, there exist a prime $p$ and a finite $p$-local spectrum $X$ such that $$\mathrm{BP}_*X\cong\mathrm{BP}_*/(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n)$$ as graded $\mathrm{BP}_*\mathrm{BP}$-comodules, with the canonical quotient coaction and generator in degree zero.*

</div>

Thus the all-height Smith–Toda existence problem has a positive answer when the prime may depend on the height. The construction takes place in ordinary $p$-local spectra, before any chromatic localization. It does not assert that one prime works at every height, provide an explicit bound on the prime, or require a multiplication on the final spectrum.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and earlier methods

The realization problem grew out of the study of complex cobordism modules and periodic phenomena in stable homotopy. Milnor’s work on complex cobordism and Novikov’s coefficient theorem established its polynomial coefficient structure (Milnor 1960; Novikov 1962) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Milnor1960](#ref-Milnor1960), [Novikov1962](#ref-Novikov1962)]. Brown and Peterson introduced the spectrum $\mathrm{BP}$ (Brown and Peterson 1966) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BrownPeterson1966](#ref-BrownPeterson1966)]. Quillen identified the formal group law underlying that theory and its $p$-typical projection (Quillen 1969) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Quillen1969](#ref-Quillen1969)]; Hazewinkel supplied the integral generators and coefficient formulas used here (Hazewinkel 1977) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[HazewinkelIII](#ref-HazewinkelIII)]. These results describe the algebraic quotients whose finite topological realization is at issue.

The low-height constructions are due to Adams, Smith, and Toda: $V(k)$ exists for $k=1,2,3$ when $p>2k$, respectively, as summarized in (Nave 2010, 493) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Nave2010](#ref-Nave2010)]. Adams’s periodicity construction is part of his work on the groups $J(X)$ (Adams 1966) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Adams1966](#ref-Adams1966)]. Smith’s work treats the realization of complex bordism modules (Smith 1970) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Smith1970](#ref-Smith1970)], while Toda constructs spectra realizing exterior parts of the Steenrod algebra (Toda 1971) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Toda1971](#ref-Toda1971)]. At height zero the Moore spectrum supplies the quotient by $p$. Each subsequent first-power attachment asks for additional topological information. In the September 2023 version of (Culver and Zhang 2024, Remark 3.29) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CulverZhang2024](#ref-CulverZhang2024)], the existence of $V(4)$ was recorded as open at every prime. We address arbitrary fixed height by allowing the prime to grow.

Known nonexistence results are consistent with this quantifier order. For $p\geq7$, Nave proves that $V((p+1)/2)$ does not exist and that $V((p-1)/2)$ cannot be a ring spectrum if it exists (Nave 2010, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Nave2010](#ref-Nave2010)]. The obstructed height depends on $p$, and the second assertion concerns additional structure. Neither is an obstruction at one fixed height at every prime.

Ultraproducts exploit the distinction between a fixed height and a varying prime. Barthel–Schlank–Stapleton establish asymptotic algebraicity in chromatically localized categories and apply it to generalized Moore objects (Barthel, Schlank, and Stapleton 2020) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BSS2020](#ref-BSS2020)]. Their compactness is in the localized category, whereas Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> requires an ordinary finite spectrum. Here the ultraproduct is formed from ordinary $p$-local spectra. We retain varying sphere degrees and construct a finite sequence of cofibers there. Only finitely many maps and homotopies must subsequently descend to an individual prime.

The other ingredients have complementary predecessors. Exactness of the fixed-prime Tate tensor power provides a model for the permutation construction (Nikolaus and Scholze 2018, Proposition III.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[NS2018](#ref-NS2018)]; our comparison requires a single Euler exponent for the entire family of tensor cubes as $p$ varies. The normalization uses completion by a perfect algebra, in the setting of the Amitsur methods of Mathew–Naumann–Noel (Mathew, Naumann, and Noel 2017) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MNN2017](#ref-MNN2017)]. Power operations in cobordism go back to tom Dieck and Quillen (Dieck 1968; Quillen 1971) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[tomDieck1968](#ref-tomDieck1968), [Quillen1971](#ref-Quillen1971)]. For detection, we use the coherent Thom construction and its Euler-product formula (May 1977; Johnson and Noel 2010) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[May1977](#ref-May1977), [JohnsonNoel2010](#ref-JohnsonNoel2010)]. We retain these powers in complex cobordism throughout the calculation, then apply the ordinary multiplicative projection to Brown–Peterson homology.

A related use of filtrations appears in Randal-Williams’s construction of Smith–Toda analogues in graded algebra modules (Randal-Williams 2026, sec. 5, Theorem 5.6 and Lemma 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[RandalWilliams2026](#ref-RandalWilliams2026)]. There successive cofibers are controlled through associated-graded generators and vanishing estimates over a field of fixed positive characteristic. The comparison here is methodological: our connectivity bounds vary with the prime and feed the normalization of an Euler layer in ordinary spectra. Recent local realization results of Kato–Shimomura–Shimomura concern quotients with a chromatic generator inverted (Kato, Shimomura, and Shimomura 2025, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[KatoShimomura2025](#ref-KatoShimomura2025)]. They address a different realization target from the ordinary finite, first-power quotient considered here.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Construction and Detection

Fix $n\geq1$ and a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ on the odd primes. The central ambient category is $$\mathcal C=\prod_{\mathcal U}\mathrm{Sp}_{(p)}.$$ Its mapping spectra are rational, although sequences of spheres in degrees growing with $p$ still carry essential information. A formal weight $w_j$ records the varying even degree $2(p^j-1)$. Mapping out of these spheres gives a rational graded algebra in which finite Koszul extensions can be formed without discarding that degree data.
